A Denotationally-based Program Logic for Higher-order Store Journal: Electronic Notes in Theoretical Informatics and Computer Science Volume: 3 Email: aagaard@cs.au.dk Email: jsterling@cs.au.dk Email: birkedal@cs.au.dk
Frederik Lerbjerg Aagaard freemail, Jonathan Sterling jonemail, Lars Birkedal laremail

Abstract
Separation logic is used to reason locally about stateful programs. State of the art program logics for higher-order store are usually built on top of untyped operational semantics, in part because traditional denotational methods have struggled to simultaneously account for general references and parametric polymorphism. The recent discovery of simple denotational semantics for general references and polymorphism in synthetic guarded domain theory has enabled us to develop Tulip , a higher-order separation logic over the typed equational theory of higher-order store for a monadic version of System 𝐅 μ 𝑟𝑒𝑓 \mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}} . The Tulip logic differs from operationally-based program logics in two ways: predicates range over the meanings of typed terms in an arbitrary model rather than over the raw code of untyped terms, and they are automatically invariant under the equational congruence of higher-order store, which applies even underneath a binder. As a result, “pure” proof steps that conventionally require focusing the Hoare triple on an operational redex are replaced by a simple equational rewrite in Tulip . We have evaluated Tulip against standard examples involving linked lists in the heap, comparing our abstract equational reasoning with more familiar operational-style reasoning. Our main result is the soundness of Tulip , which we establish by constructing a BI-hyperdoctrine over the denotational semantics of 𝐅 μ 𝑟𝑒𝑓 \mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}} in an impredicative version of synthetic guarded domain theory.



keywords

denotational semantics, higher-order separation logic, higher-order store, general references, guarded recursion, synthetic guarded domain theory, BI-hyperdoctrine, impredicative polymorphism, recursive types

1
Introduction

State of the art program logics such as Iris
[
30
]
and the Verified
Software Toolchain
[
1
]
typically combine two design decisions:
the basic sorts of the logic are generated by the
raw, untyped terms
of
the programming language, and constructs of the program logic are defined in
terms of operational semantics layered over this raw syntax. In contrast, the
original program logic
LCF
[
24
,
41
,
39
,
23
]

and successors like HOLCF
[
27
]
work in a more abstract way:
predicates range over
typed denotations
rather than over untyped raw
syntax. The difference in abstraction is substantial: whereas a predicate on
raw syntax need not even be invariant under
\alpha
-equivalence, a predicate
on denotations is automatically invariant under the
entire
equational
theory of the language. Nevertheless, denotationally-based program logics have
fallen by the wayside in part because of the historic failure of denotational
methods to provide simple enough answers to the questions posed by higher-order
effectful programming,
e.g
.
the combination of general references, polymorphism,
and concurrency; as a result, the benefits of denotations are primarily reaped
today in the first-order realm, as in the successful revival of coinductive
resumption semantics by Xia
et al
.

[
47
]
.

Denotational and algebraic approaches to program semantics have other benefits
besides their abstractness: they are more modular, and they are more directly
compatible with practical mathematical tools from category theory and topology,
which have become increasingly relevant in light of recent interest in
probabilistic and differentiable computing for the sciences. We therefore have
ample motivation to bridge the gap between denotational methods and modern
logics for reasoning about programs.

1.1
Denotational semantics of general references and polymorphism

Sterling
et al
.

[
46
]
have recently discovered a simple
denotational semantics for general references and polymorphism, decomposing the
problem of higher-order store with semantic worlds into two separate constructs
that are easily combined: impredicativity and guarded recursion. At the heart
of their development is
Impredicative Guarded Dependent Type Theory

(
iGDTT
), a type theoretic metalanguage for synthetic guarded domain
theory
[
11
]
extended by an impredicative universe of sets.
Although
op. cit
.
have made promising strides in the denotational understanding
of higher-order stateful programs, non-trivial
reasoning
about state
requires more than equational logic. Operationally-based program logics like
Iris employ
guarded higher-order separation logic
for this purpose; our
contribution in this paper is to adapt these methods to the denotational semantics of
Sterling
et al
.

1.2
Higher-order separation logic over denotational semantics

We extend the
monadic equational theory
of higher-order store for
System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
to a full higher-order separation logic called
Tulip
. In
contrast to systems like Iris which layer the separation logic over an
encoding of the
raw syntax
of the object language, predicates in

Tulip
range not over code but rather over the actual types of
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
,
subject to the full equational congruence. In a conventional
operationally-based program logic, equational reasoning is rather only applicable
within the focus of a Hoare triple and is moreover limited to operational head
reduction and cannot occur under binders,
etc
.

Our main contribution is the soundness of
Tulip
for reasoning about
Sterling
et al
.
’s denotational semantics of
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
, which we establish by
constructing a suitable
BI-hyperdoctrine
[
8
]
. We have made
pervasive use of type theoretic internal languages to facilitate this work:
first, System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
is intepreted in
internal co-presheaves
on a
poset of semantic heap layouts
\mathbb{W}
defined in
iGDTT
by solving a guarded
recursive domain equation; then, our BI-hyperdoctrine is itself constructed in
the type theoretic language of co-presheaves on
\mathbb{W}
. Because
iGDTT

itself is sound
[
46
]
, it follows that our logic is sound.

1.3
Structure of this paper

In
Section

2
we describe the
Tulip
logic and its computational
substrate
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
; in
Section

3
, we illustrate the use of
Tulip

through a worked example involving linked lists in the heap. In

Section

4.1
, we recall the
iGDTT
metalanguage and the basics of
synthetic guarded domain theory, which we use in

Sections

4.2
and
4.3
to explain the
denotational semantics of higher-order store and the interpretation of guarded
higher-order separation logic over this model. In
Section

4.4
, we show
that
Tulip
is sound with respect to this model and therefore consistent.

2
Tulip
: an equational program logic for higher-order store

In this section, we introduce a
Tulip
program logic that extends the
monadic equational logic of System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
with the connectives
and rules of guarded higher-order separation
logic
[
13
]
, together with a built-in connective for

weakest preconditions
. The
Tulip
logic is related to System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
in roughly the
same way that
LCF
relates to
PCF

[
41
,
44
]
.

Tulip
will have two kinds of types —
“program types”
and

“logical types”
— and to efficiently organize the formalism, we
will define the typehood judgment schematically in a flag

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\iota\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}},\boldsymbol{\ell}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}},\boldsymbol{\ell}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}},\boldsymbol{\ell}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}},\boldsymbol{\ell}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}
.
1
1

1

Both System F
and

higher-order logic are modeled by impredicative universes, but it is well-known
that one impredicative universe cannot contain another
[
17
]
,
which leads us to introduce the stratification of logical types from program types. The prime example of a logical
type that is not a program type will be the type of all propositions.
With
this convention in hand, the forms of
judgment of
Tulip
classify type contexts

\Xi\ \textit{tctx}

,

element contexts

\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}

,
types

\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}

,
program elements

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash a:A

,
propositions

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}

, and entailments

\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\phi\vdash\psi

.
Every syntactic construct
of
Tulip
is subject to a relation of
judgmental equality

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\equiv$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\equiv$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\equiv$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\equiv$}\mathclose{}{)}}
which is respected by all judgments and preserved by all
operations; for instance, we shall write
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u\equiv v:A}
to
mean that
u
and
v
are judgmentally equal elements of type
A
.

Notation
\thethm

When specifying the rules of
Tulip
, we will often write entailments

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\phi\vdash\psi}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\phi\vdash\psi}

when the contexts do not change from premise to conclusion.

2.1
Rules for types and elements

Type contexts
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\Xi\ \textit{tctx}}
contain variables
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\alpha}
ranging over

program types
only. Element contexts
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}}
contain
variables
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}x:A}
ranging over elements of types

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}}
. Both program types and logical types are
closed under function spaces, cartesian products, inductive types, and universal quantification over program types; program
types are furthermore closed under a computational monad in the sense of
Moggi
[
36
]
, as well as existential and recursive
types. The only subtlety is that the
unfold
destructor for recursive
types is treated
effectfully
, confining recursion to the
monad. Every program type is a logical type, and the inclusion of program types
into logical types commutes up to isomorphism with function spaces, product
types, and inductive types. We will treat these coercions
silently as it causes no ambiguity.

For lack of space, we will not dwell on the product and function types except
to comment that they satisfy the full universal properties of exponentials and
cartesian products up to judgmental equality. Likewise, we do not present here
the (quite standard) account of inductive types — except to note that we
close both program and logical types under inductives, and structurally
recursive functions into logical types on program-level data are permitted.

2.1.1
The computational monad

The universe of program types is closed under a computational monad
\mathtt{T}
in the
sense of Moggi
[
36
]
governing both state and general recursion.

\frac{\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}{\Xi\vdash\mathtt{T}\,A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{ret}\,u:\mathtt{T}\,A}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\mathtt{T}\,A\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:A\vdash v:\mathtt{T}\,B}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash x\leftarrow u;v:\mathtt{T}\,B}

We present the standard equational theory of monads in

Appendix

A
. We defer our discussion of the monadic operations
for general recursion and state to
Section

2.1.2
.

2.1.2
General recursion and general references

Program types are closed under both general recursive types and general
reference types. As the rules for these are somewhat subtle in our monadic
environment, we cover them in detail. First we describe the formation rules for
types and terms, before introducing their equational theory.

\frac{\Xi,\alpha\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}{\Xi\vdash\mu\alpha.A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{fold}\,u:\mu\alpha.A}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\mu\alpha.A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{unfold}\,u:\mathtt{T}\,{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}\\
\frac{\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}{\Xi\vdash\mathtt{ref}\,A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\mathtt{ref}\,A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{get}\,u:\mathtt{T}\,A}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\mathtt{ref}\,A\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash v:A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{set}\,u\,v:\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{new}\,u:\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ref}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ref}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ref}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ref}\,A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\and{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\frac{}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{step}:\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}

The
\mathtt{step}
constructor above is a “no-op” instruction witnessing the

guarded
or
intensional
nature of our denotational
semantics;
2
2

2

In the presence of polymorphism, the guarded interpretation
of state and general recursion seems to be
forced
[
12
]
.
this is reflected in the
equational theory by the emission of
\mathtt{step}
instructions with reads to the
heap and the unfolding of recursive types. Our logic, as we explain later,
will provide rules that allow these steps to be used as fuel for recursive
deductions.

\frac{}{\vdash\mathtt{unfold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{fold}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{fold}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{step}};\mathtt{ret}\,{u}:\mathtt{T}\,A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}\quad\textsc{unfold-of-fold}\and\frac{}{\vdash{x\leftarrow\mathtt{unfold}\,u;\mathtt{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{fold}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{fold}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{step}};\mathtt{ret}\,u:\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\quad\textsc{fold-of-unfold}\and\frac{}{\vdash\mathtt{set}\,u\,v;\mathtt{get}\,u\equiv{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathtt{step}};\mathtt{set}\,u\,v;\mathtt{ret}\,v:\mathtt{T}\,A}\quad\textsc{get-after-set}\and\frac{}{\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,x\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,x\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,x\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,x\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{new}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{T}\,B}\quad\textsc{set-after-new}\and\frac{}{\vdash\mathtt{set}\,u\,v;\mathtt{set}\,u\,w\equiv\mathtt{set}\,u\,w:\mathtt{T}\,A}\quad\textsc{set-after-set}\and\frac{}{\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,v;x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{T}\,C}\quad\textsc{get-after-get}\and\frac{}{\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,u\,x;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,u\,x;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,u\,x;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;\mathtt{set}\,u\,x;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,u;w$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{T}\,B}\quad\textsc{set-after-get}

We also have, but do not present, rules that commute
\mathtt{step}
past all
primitive effects.

Remark 2.1
.

Observe that ours is a theory of
higher-order global
store
rather than
higher-order local store
in the sense that
allocations are not hidden but rather have a globally observable effect. The state of the art in denotational
semantics for local store is currently restricted to references of
ground
type

[
31
]
.

2.1.3
Universal and existential types

Our language contains both universal and existential types; it is common to
‘‘encode’’ the latter in terms of the former, but encodings of this form are
not quite correct as they neglect the equational theory of existential types.
3
3

3

The correct equational theory of polymorphically-encoded existentials does hold up to parametricity, but parametricity is an emergent property of
syntax
. The purpose of specifying an equational theory is to constrain
all
models, not only the nonstandard parametric models.

We therefore include both connectives as primitives.

\frac{\Xi,\alpha\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}}{\Xi\vdash\forall\alpha.A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}}\and\frac{\Xi,\alpha\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}{\Xi\vdash\exists\alpha.A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}\and\frac{\Xi,\alpha\mid\Gamma\vdash u:A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\Lambda\alpha.u:\forall\alpha.A}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\forall\alpha.A\quad\Xi\vdash B\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u\cdot B:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}\and\frac{\Xi\vdash B\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{pack}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle B,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle B,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle B,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\exists\alpha.A}\and\frac{\Xi\vdash C\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u:\exists\alpha.A\quad\Xi,\alpha\mid\Gamma,x:A\vdash v:C}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{let}\ \mathtt{pack}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}=u\ \mathtt{in}\ v:C}

The equational theory of universals and existentials is presented in
Appendix

A
.

2.2
Rules for the propositional fragment

The logical layer of
Tulip
is a form of
guarded higher-order
separation logic
. We will treat each of these aspects modularly; in

Sections

2.2.1
and
2.2.2
we recall the rules of intuitionistic
higher-order equational logic, and in
Section

2.2.3
we recall
(affine) separation logic, and we finish in
Section

2.2.4
with an
overview of the
later modality
and its Löb induction principle, and
how they interact with the rest of the logic.

2.2.1
Equational logic

So far the only form of equality that we have considered is
judgmental

or
external equality
in the sense of Jacobs
[
29
, §3.2]
;
in order to facilitate equational reasoning in the logic, we add propositional
equality and relate it to judgmental equality using the following rules:

\frac{\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u,v:A}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u\mathrel{=_{A}}v\ \textit{prop}}\quad\textsc{equality formation}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:A\mid\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}\vdash\psi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x/y$}\mathclose{}{]}}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:A,y:A\mid\phi\land x\mathrel{=_{A}}y\vdash\psi}\quad\textsc{Lawvere rule}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\top\vdash u\mathrel{=_{A}}v}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash u\equiv v:A}\quad\textsc{equality reflection}

As Jacobs
[
29
]
explains, the
Lawvere
rule entails all
important properties of equality, including congruence for all constructs of

Tulip
and its
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
substrate. The
equality reflection

rule above is needed to complete the relationship between (unconditional)
judgmental equality and propositional equality without
assumptions.
4
4

4

Ordinarily, equality reflection would imply the

Lawvere
rule but for the lack of propositional assumptions in
judgmental equality.

2.2.2
Intuitionistic higher-order logic

We assume the usual rules of intuitionistic first-order logic over logical types; in
particular, in addition to implications
\Rightarrow
, conjunctions
\land
,
and disjunctions
\lor
, we may form universal and existential quantifications

\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\phi
and
\exists\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:A$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\phi
when
A
is a logical type.
The logic is made higher-order by introducing a logical type classifying all
propositions.

\frac{}{\Xi\vdash\mathtt{prop}\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\ell}}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi:\mathtt{prop}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\equiv\psi:\mathtt{prop}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\equiv\psi\ \textit{prop}}\and\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\chi\land\phi\vdash\psi\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\chi\land\psi\vdash\phi}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\chi\vdash\phi\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{prop}}}\psi}

2.2.3
Separation logic for local reasoning

We assume the standard rules for intuitionistic affine separation logic, in
which we have a separating conjunction
\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi
with unit
\top
, and
separating implications given as right adjoints
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}\dashv\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\psi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\psi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\psi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\psi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}-$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

\frac{\chi\mathrel{\ast}\phi\vdash\psi}{\chi\vdash\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi}\and\frac{\phi\vdash\phi^{\prime}\quad\psi\vdash\psi^{\prime}}{\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi\vdash\phi^{\prime}\mathrel{\ast}\psi^{\prime}}\and\frac{}{\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi\vdash\phi\land\psi\\
\\
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\chi\mathrel{\ast}\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\chi\mathrel{\ast}\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\chi\mathrel{\ast}\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi\mathrel{\ast}\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}\mathrel{\ast}\psi\mathrel{\dashv\vdash}\chi\mathrel{\ast}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}\\
\\
\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi\mathrel{\dashv\vdash}\psi\mathrel{\ast}\phi}\and

In addition to the separating conjunction and implication, separation logic
contains a
coreflective sublogic
of
persistent propositions
,
which are to a first approximation those that are not sensitive to the state of
the heap and can therefore be duplicated freely. In particular, we add an
idempotent comonadic modality
\square
that takes a proposition to its
“persistent core”; a proposition
\phi
is then called persistent if the
entailment
\phi\vdash\square\phi
holds. Persistent propositions in this sense
are closed under all the connectives of intuitionistic first-order logic; we
omit the rules that establish this and instead focus on the interaction between
persistence and the connectives of separation logic:

\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\square\phi\ \textit{prop}}\and\frac{\phi\vdash\psi}{\square\phi\vdash\square\psi}\and\frac{}{\square\phi\vdash\phi\mathrel{\ast}\square\phi\\
\\
\square\phi\vdash\square\square\phi\\
\\
\phi\land\square\psi\vdash\phi\mathrel{\ast}\square\psi}

It follows from the above that the separating conjunction of persistent
propositions is their conjunction.

2.2.4
The later modality and guarded recursion

In order to use
Tulip
as a logic to reason about general recursion
(including recursion inherent in the heap), it is necessary to introduce the

later modality

\rhd
; as in prior
works
[
2
,
21
,
30
]
, the
later modality abstracts away the onerous step-indices of more concrete
accounts of higher-order store leaving only the essential logical structure of
guarded-recursive reasoning. The abstract will meet the concrete, however, when
we illustrate below the interaction between the later modality and the constructions
of our programming language in the
fold equality
and
step equality

rules.

\frac{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\rhd\phi\ \textit{prop}}\and\frac{}{\phi\vdash\rhd{\phi}\\
\\
\rhd\phi\land\rhd\psi\vdash\rhd\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\land\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\land\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\land\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\land\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\and\frac{}{\rhd\phi\mathrel{\ast}\rhd\psi\vdash\rhd\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\mathrel{\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}\\
\\
\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi\vdash\rhd\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\rhd\psi\\
\\
\rhd\square\phi\mathrel{\dashv\vdash}\square{\rhd\phi}}\\
\frac{\phi\vdash\rhd\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\mathrel{=_{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\mathrel{=_{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\mathrel{=_{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\mathrel{=_{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu\alpha.A/\alpha$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{\phi\vdash\mathtt{fold}\,u\mathrel{=_{\mu\alpha.A}}\mathtt{fold}\,v}\quad\textsc{fold equality}\and\frac{\phi\vdash\rhd\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{T}\,A}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{T}\,A}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{T}\,A}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{T}\,A}}v$}\mathclose{}{)}}}{\phi\vdash\mathtt{step};u\mathrel{=_{\mathtt{T}\,A}}\mathtt{step};v}\quad\textsc{step equality}\and\frac{}{\rhd\phi\Rightarrow\phi\vdash\phi}\quad\textsc{L\"{o}b induction}

The
Löb induction
rule above is what makes (guarded) recursive
reasoning possible in
Tulip
; the function of the
fold equality
and

step equality
rules is to provide “fuel” that can be used to
discharge the later modality in the Löb induction hypothesis. This is the
sense in which
Tulip
evinces an abstract form of step-indexing: operations
that semantically involve unfolding a recursive domain equation leave behind
abstract steps that can be used to advance in time in relation to the later modality.

2.2.5
Weakest preconditions

For reasoning about programs, we introduce a connective called the

partial weakest precondition
. Morally, the weakest precondition of a
program and a predicate, as the name suggests, is the weakest proposition that
guarantees the predicate shall hold of any return value of the program. Note,
however, that, despite the name, we make no claim, neither in the logic nor in
its semantics, that it is in fact the weakest such proposition; this is in line
with the usage in Iris
[
30
]
.

\frac{\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash e:\mathtt{T}\,A\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:A\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,e\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \textit{prop}}\quad\textsc{wp formation}\and\frac{}{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\forall x.\,\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\forall x.\,\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\forall x.\,\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\forall x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\hookrightarrow e$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\hookrightarrow e$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\hookrightarrow e$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\hookrightarrow e$}\mathclose{}{]}}\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\phi\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{new}\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{new}\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{new}\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{new}\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\quad\textsc{wp-new}\and\frac{}{\rhd\phi\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,\mathtt{step}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}}\quad\textsc{wp-step}

It is worth comparing the above rules to the rules in
Iris
[
30
, Fig.13]
. Our first four entailments are exactly the
same as the corresponding rules in Iris; note that as in Iris,

wp-wand
implies the frame rule. Our rules for
wp-set
and

wp-new
rules differ slightly from those of Iris, which have an
occurrence of the later modality in the antecedent that ours lack. This is
because every operation in Iris takes a step, but in our semantics, only
operations that semantically correspond to unfolding a recursive domain
equation do.

Values in the heap are stored “one step in the future”, so
\mathtt{get}
must take
a step before returning; consequently, the postcondition of
\mathtt{get}
only needs
to hold
later
. However, it is not an issue for
\mathtt{set}
and
\mathtt{new}
to
send a value to the future without going there, implying the postcondition must
be known now. This behaviour is reflected in the rules
get-after-set

and
set-after-new
, where the former shows that
\mathtt{get}
takes a step,
whilst
\mathtt{ret}
does not, and the latter shows that
\mathtt{set}
does not take a
step.
A second difference to point out is that whilst Iris has a rule for

\beta
-reduction of functions, we do not. This is because our programming
language is subject to the
\beta/\eta
-equational theory of monadic

\lambda
-calculus, so rather than having a rule stating that
\mathtt{wp}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle e\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle e\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle e\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle e\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v/x$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda x.\,e$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y.\,\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
, the two propositions are actually
convertible
.

Example 2.2
.

It is not difficult to encode the more familiar
Hoare triples
in
Tulip
,
using the standard decomposition into persistence, separating implication, and weakest precondition:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\,e\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\triangleq\square\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\mathtt{wp}\,e\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\mathtt{wp}\,e\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,\psi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\mathtt{wp}\,e\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 2.3
Recursive Functions

As is standard, recursive types can be used to derive recursive terms via
self-referential types (see
e.g
.
Harper
[
25
]
). Using this approach, we
obtain terms
\mathtt{rec}\,f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ \mathtt{in}\ e
for recursive functions typed as follows:

\frac{\Xi\vdash A,B\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}\quad\Xi\mid\Gamma,f:A\to\mathtt{T}\,B,x:A\vdash e:\mathtt{T}\,B}{\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{rec}\,f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ \mathtt{in}\ e:A\to\mathtt{T}\,B}

3
Case study: verifying the
append
function on linked lists

In this section, we illustrate the use of
Tulip
by an elementary case
study: linked lists in the heap and their append function. The proof is very
similar to the one in Iris
[
9
, §4.2]
, although
some steps are perhaps slightly simpler as they use equational reasoning rather
than explicit rules for reduction in weakest preconditions. We first define a
recursive type of imperative linked lists on any type
\alpha
:

\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\triangleq\mu\rho.\mathtt{1}+\mathtt{ref}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\times\rho$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\times\rho$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\times\rho$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\times\rho$}\mathclose{}{)}}.

Our goal is to define the
append
function on imperative linked lists and
prove that it is correct. This means, in particular, to show that it behaves
the same as a pure
reference implementation
defined on functional lists.
In order to say what it means for a function on imperative lists to behave like
a function on linked lists, we must first introduce a formal correspondence
between the two types
[
9
, Sec.4.2]
. This can be
done directly as a structurally recursive function in
Tulip
.

Construction 1

(The list invariant)

We define a correspondence
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{list}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{prop}
in

Tulip
by structural recursion on the second argument.

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\approx}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{list}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{prop}

l\mathrel{\approx}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l=\mathtt{fold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{inl}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{inl}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{inl}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{inl}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Note that whilst elements of type
\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha
could potentially be cyclic,
this is ruled out by the list invariant above: the separating conjunction
consumes the location
r
so it cannot appear again, and furthermore, a cyclic
list would be infinite and therefore cannot correspond to a functional list.

Construction 2

(The
append
function)

We define the append function on linked lists and its pure reference
implementation on functional lists below.

\mathtt{iap}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\times\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathtt{iap}\triangleq

\mathtt{rec}\,f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ \mathtt{in}\ {}

z\leftarrow\mathtt{unfold}\,l_{1};

\mathtt{match}\,z\,\mathtt{with}

\mathtt{inl}\,\_\Rightarrow\mathtt{ret}\,l_{2}

\mathtt{inr}\,r\Rightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,l_{1}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,l_{1}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,l_{1}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,l_{1}^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\leftarrow\mathtt{get}\,r;l_{3}\leftarrow f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1}^{\prime},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1}^{\prime},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1}^{\prime},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1}^{\prime},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathtt{set}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a,l_{3}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a,l_{3}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a,l_{3}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a,l_{3}$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathtt{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{fold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{fold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{fold}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{inr}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

The function above is both impure and general recursive. By contrast, the
reference implementation below is pure and structurally recursive.

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathtt{list}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{list}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{list}\,\alpha

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\,$}\mathclose{}{]}}\oplus\mathit{ys}\triangleq\mathit{ys}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\mathbin{::}\mathit{xs}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\mathbin{::}\mathit{xs}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\mathbin{::}\mathit{xs}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\mathbin{::}\mathit{xs}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\oplus\mathit{ys}\triangleq x\mathbin{::}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathit{xs}\oplus\mathit{ys}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathit{xs}\oplus\mathit{ys}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathit{xs}\oplus\mathit{ys}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathit{xs}\oplus\mathit{ys}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We can now prove that
\mathtt{iap}
behaves according to the reference
implementation
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\oplus$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

The following sequent is derivable in

:

Proof 3.2
.

Let
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
be the function defined so
that
\mathtt{iap}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathtt{rec}\,f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\ \mathtt{in}\ {\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}
,
and let
Q
be the following predicate:

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\times\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\times\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\times\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\times\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha\to\mathtt{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathtt{prop}

\displaystyle Q\,f

\displaystyle\triangleq\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u_{1},u_{2}:\mathtt{list}\,\alpha;l_{1},l_{2}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u_{1},u_{2}:\mathtt{list}\,\alpha;l_{1},l_{2}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u_{1},u_{2}:\mathtt{list}\,\alpha;l_{1},l_{2}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u_{1},u_{2}:\mathtt{list}\,\alpha;l_{1},l_{2}:\mathtt{ilist}\,\alpha$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,l_{1}\mathrel{\approx}u_{1}\mathrel{\ast}\l_{2}\mathrel{\approx}u_{2}\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\mathtt{wp}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l_{1},l_{2}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\,x\mathrel{\approx}{u_{1}\oplus u_{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\,x\mathrel{\approx}{u_{1}\oplus u_{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\,x\mathrel{\approx}{u_{1}\oplus u_{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\,x\mathrel{\approx}{u_{1}\oplus u_{2}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

Our goal is to prove
Q\,\mathtt{iap}
; applying the
wp-rec
rule, it
suffices to show that
Q\,\mathtt{iap}\vdash Q\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.
We proceed by cases on
u_{1}
; the only non-trivial
case is the following:

Q\,\mathtt{iap}\mathrel{\ast}l_{1}\mathrel{\approx}v\mathbin{::}\mathit{vs}\mathrel{\ast}l_{2}\mathrel{\approx}u_{2}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{iap}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\,\mathtt{iap}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 4
Denotational semantics of
Tulip
in impredicative guarded dependent type theory

The denotational semantics of
Tulip
is an extension of the model of
System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
previously constructed by Sterling, Gratzer, and
Birkedal
[
46
]
. For lack of space, we can only
give a brief introduction to the latter, focusing on the main ideas. The main
ingredient to our semantics is the use of
impredicative guarded
dependent type theory
(
iGDTT
) as a sufficiently powerful metalanguage to
admit both the synthetic solution of domain equations for recursively defined
semantic worlds (which uses guarded recursion)
and
the definition of the
store-passing monad (which uses impredicativity). In
Section

4.1
we give a
brief overview of
iGDTT
, and we proceed in
Section

4.2
to
explain the interpretation of higher-order store.

4.1
Impredicative guarded dependent type theory

Impredicative guarded dependent type theory or
iGDTT
is roughly the
extension of extensional
guarded dependent type
theory

[
14
,
15
]
by additional (impredicative)
universe structure. We first describe the universes in

Section

4.1.1
, and then briefly explain the basics of
iGDTT
’s
synthetic guarded domain theory in

Sections

4.1.2
and
4.1.3
. Finally, we describe a simple
recipe for constructing models of
iGDTT
in
Section

4.1.4
from
which consistency immediately follows.

4.1.1
Impredicative universes

iGDTT
adds to ordinary guarded dependent type theory the following
additional universe structure:

(1)

We assume an ordinary hierarchy of predicative universes
\mathsf{Type}_{0}:\mathsf{Type}_{1}:\ldots
; when it causes no confusion, we will write
\mathsf{Type}
for any
appropriate
\mathsf{Type}_{i}
.

(2)

We further assume a pair of impredicative universes
\mathsf{Set},\mathsf{Prop}:\mathsf{Type}_{0}

of small types and proof-irrelevant propositions respectively, where the
latter satisfies propositional extensionality.
5
5

5

Note that
\mathsf{Prop}

is not an element of
\mathsf{Set}
, as this would be
inconsistent
[
17
]
.
Finally, we assume that any element of

\mathsf{Prop}
is also an element of
\mathsf{Set}
.

The universe structure above is roughly that of the
\mathtt{Set}
and

\mathtt{Prop}
universes of Coq
[
16
]
underneath

\mathtt{Type}
when the
-impredicative-set
option is activated.
Impredicativity of
\mathsf{Set}
means closure under dependent products

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{B}:\mathsf{Set}}
of “large-indexed” families
x:A\vdash B:\mathsf{Set}
when
A:\mathsf{Type}
, and likewise for
\mathsf{Prop}
. The coherent
impredicative encoding of Awodey, Frey, and
Speight
[
6
]
ensures that the full internal
subcategory determined by
\mathsf{Set}
is
reflective
in
\mathsf{Type}
, and so
a genuine existential
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}B:\mathsf{Set}}
can be obtained by applying
the reflection to the (large) dependent sum
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{B}:\mathsf{Type}}
. On

\mathsf{Prop}
these are exactly the ordinary universal and existential quantifiers
— as the reflection is the “bracket
type” of Awodey and Bauer
[
5
]
.

In what follows, we shall let
\mathcal{U}
stand for any of the universes of

iGDTT
described here.

4.1.2
The later modality and guarded recursion

Every universe
\mathcal{U}
is closed under a
“later modality”
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathord{\blacktriangleright}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}}
facilitating guarded recursion.
The later modality also satisfies a dependently typed version of the rules of
an applicative functor. Although there are many ways to present this structure,
we choose to follow prior
work
[
14
,
15
,
46
]
by
formulating them using
delayed substitutions

\delta\leadsto\Delta

, which we describe simultaneously with the rules
of the later modality:

\frac{\delta\leadsto\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash A\ \mathit{type}}{\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A\ \mathit{type}}\quad\textsc{later formation}\and\frac{\delta\leadsto\Delta\quad\Delta\vdash a:A}{\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{a}:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{A}}\quad\textsc{later e\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.a=\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.a^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a=a^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a=a^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a=a^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a=a^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\quad\textsc{later id}\and\frac{\delta\leadsto\Delta\quad a:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A}{\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\delta,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\delta,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\delta,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\delta,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}.x=a}\quad\textsc{next variable}

Then the ordinary later modality
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}
sends
A:\mathcal{U}
to
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A

via the empty delayed substitution; likewise, we shall write
\mathsf{next}\,{a}
for

\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}.a
. From these rules, we may deduce that the later modality
forms a
well-pointed endofunctor

\mathord{\blacktriangleright}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}
in the sense of
Kelly
[
32
]
, and moreover preserves cartesian products.
The later modality also comes
equipped with a
Löb recursor
for defining guarded fixed points as
specified below:

\frac{}{\mathsf{gfix}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A}\quad\textsc{L\"{o}b recursor}\and\frac{}{\mathsf{gfix}\,f=f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\quad\textsc{L\"{o}b unfolding}

4.1.3
Basic synthetic guarded domain theory

Definition 4.1
.

A
guarded domain
in
\mathcal{U}
is defined to be an algebra for the
endofunctor
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}
,
i.e
.
a type
X:\mathcal{U}
equipped with a function

\vartheta_{X}:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A
. A homomorphism from
X
to
Y
is then given by
a function
f:X\to Y
that commutes with the algebra maps in the sense that

\vartheta_{Y}\circ\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{f}=f\circ\vartheta_{X}
. We will write
\mathcal{U}^{\mathord{\blacktriangleright}}
for the
category of guarded domains in
\mathcal{U}
and their homomorphisms.

Example 4.2
.

The universe
\mathcal{U}
is a guarded domain in any higher universe
\mathcal{V}
as we may
define
\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}\to\mathcal{U}
to send
A:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}
to the
delayed type
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}.Z
using the unary delayed substitution

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\leadsto Z:\mathcal{U}}
.

Following Birkedal and Møgelberg
[
10
]
, the Löb
recursor can be used to solve domain equations by computing fixed
points on the universe
\mathcal{U}
. The simplest example of a guarded domain equation
is the one that defines the
guarded lift functor

\mathsf{L}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathord{\blacktriangleright}}
of Paviotti, Møgelberg, and
Birkedal
[
40
]
which sends a type to the free
guarded domain on that type,
i.e
.
the left adjoint to the forgetful functor from
guarded domains to types. The domain equation in question is
\mathsf{L}{A}=A+\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathsf{L}{A}}
, which we solve using the Löb recursor on the universe
together with the latter’s guarded domain structure:

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathsf{gfix}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda X:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}.A+\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\lambda X:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}.A+\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\lambda X:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}.A+\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}X$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\lambda X:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{U}}.A+\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}X$}\mathclose{}{)}}=A+\vartheta_{\mathcal{U}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}=A+\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle Z\leftarrow\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle Z\leftarrow\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Z\leftarrow\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Z\leftarrow\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}.Z=A+\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathsf{L}{A}}

The algebra structure
\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}{A}}:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathsf{L}{A}}\to\mathsf{L}{A}
is given by the right-hand coproduct injection;
the left-hand injection then defines the unit map
\eta:A\to\mathsf{L}{A}
for
the monad determined by the resulting adjunction between guarded domains and
types.

Construction 3

(Guarded domains are lift-algebras)

Any guarded domain
X
is also an algebra for the monad
\mathsf{L}
. As

\mathsf{L}{X}
is the
free

\mathord{\blacktriangleright}
-algebra on
X
, there is a unique
homomorphism of
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}
-algebras
\alpha_{X}:\mathsf{L}{X}\to X
such that

\alpha_{X}\circ\eta=\mathsf{id}_{X}
which induces an

\mathsf{L}
-algebra structure on
X
.

Construction 4

(Family lifting for the guarded lift monad)

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a universe, and let
A:\mathsf{Type}
be a type. We may lift a
family
\Phi:A\to\mathcal{U}
to a family
\Phi^{\mathsf{L}}:\mathsf{L}{A}\to\mathcal{U}

defined using the induced
\mathsf{L}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{U}
,
i.e
.

\Phi^{\mathsf{L}}\triangleq\alpha_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\mathsf{L}{\Phi}
.
We will occasionally write
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}u\Downarrow\Phi}
to mean
\Phi^{\mathsf{L}}u
.

4.1.4
Consistency and models of
iGDTT

A simple and modular recipe for constructing non-trivial models of
iGDTT
is
provided by Sterling, Gratzer, and
Birkedal
[
46
]
, from which consistency is
easily deduced.

Theorem 4.3

(S., Gratzer, and B.
[
46
]
)
.

Let
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a
separated intuitionistic
well-founded poset
6
6

6

We omit the definition of
