4.2
Denotational semantics of general store in
iGDTT

We briefly recall the denotational semantics of general store in
iGDTT
via a

presheaf model
. We will first construct a preorder
\mathbb{W}
of semantic
worlds (representing heap layouts), and then we shall interpret
program
types
as
\mathsf{Set}
-valued co-presheaves on
\mathbb{W}
and
logical types

as
\mathsf{Type}
-valued co-presheaves on
\mathbb{W}
.

Notation 5

When
P
is a partial order and
p\leq_{P}q
, we will write
q_{*}:Ep\to Eq

for the covariant functorial action of any functor
E:P\to\mathscr{E}
.

4.2.1
Recursively defined semantic worlds

In this section, we will define a partial order
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}
of
semantic worlds simultaneously with the collection of semantic types by
solving a guarded domain equation in
\mathsf{Type}
. We will ultimately define

\mathbb{W}
to be a kind of finite mapping of locations to types, but we must
be more careful than usual because notions like “finite subtype” are
somewhat sensitive when
\mathsf{Prop}
is not a true subobject classifier.

Definition 4.6
.

Let
I
be a totally ordered type; a
finite subtype

U\mathrel{\subseteq_{\textit{fin.}}}I
is an element
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}:\mathbb{N}
together with a monotone
injective
7
7

7

We mean injective in the general intuitionistic sense:
elements of the domain are equal if and only if they are identified by the
function in question.
function
\sigma_{U}:\mathbb{N}_{<\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}\hookrightarrow I
. We
will often abuse notation by writing
U
to refer to the image of

\sigma_{U}
in
I
. There is a (decidable) preorder on finite subtypes given
by inclusion, which is in fact a partial order because of the monotonicity of

\sigma_{U}
in the total order
I
.

Definition 4.7
.

Given a totally ordered type
I
, a
finite mapping

w:I\mathbin{\rightharpoonup_{\textit{fin.}}}T

is given by a finite subtype
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\mathrel{\subseteq_{\textit{fin.}}}I
called the

support
together with a function
\tau_{w}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\to T
called
the
labeling
. Given
i\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
we shall simply write
wi:T

for
\tau_{w}i
.
There is a partial order on finite mappings
w:I\mathbin{\rightharpoonup_{\textit{fin.}}}T
given by
inclusion of supports: we say that
w\leq w^{\prime}
when
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\leq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}

and the restriction of
\tau_{w^{\prime}}
to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
is equal to
\tau_{w}
.
Note that the partial order on finite mappings is not decidable unless
T

has decidable equality.

We can now use the notion of finite mapping to define a partial order of

semantic worlds

\mathbb{W}
simultaneously with the categories

\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}},\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
of semantic program types and semantic logical types
respectively by solving the following guarded domain equation:

\mathbb{W}=\mathbb{N}\mathbin{\rightharpoonup_{\textit{fin.}}}\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}}\qquad\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\qquad\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

In the above, we have defined a world to be a finite mapping from memory
locations to delayed semantic program types, which are defined to be

\mathsf{Set}
-valued co-presheaves
on the poset of semantic worlds.
Semantic logical types are defined similarly as
\mathsf{Type}
-valued
co-presheaves.

The impredicativity of
\mathsf{Set}
is essential for
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}
to be cartesian
closed and therefore capable of modelling function types. This is because exponentials
of co-presheaves are defined from natural transformations. In
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}
, these
are
\mathbb{W}
-indexed, and thus, since
\mathbb{W}
is not
\mathsf{Set}
-small, were it
not for the impredicativity of
\mathsf{Set}
, the homsets would also not be
\mathsf{Set}
-small.
The cartesian closure of
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
follows simply from
\mathbb{W}
being

\mathsf{Type}
-small.

Observation 6

Note that both
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
are guarded domains in the sense
of
Definition

4.1
: the structure map

\vartheta_{\mathcal{S}_{\iota}}
sends
A:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{S}_{\iota}
to
the co-presheaf
w\mapsto\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Z\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}.Zw
.

4.2.2

The semantic notion of
heap
or
memory
can be specified in terms
of the more general
heaplet distributor
on
\mathbb{W}
. This is the

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathcal{H}:\mathbb{W}^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathbb{W}\to\mathsf{Set}}
that classifies heaps whose layout is
governed by the contravariant parameter and whose values vary in the covariant
parameter.

\mathcal{H}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{-},w^{+}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{-},w^{+}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Using the notion of a heaplet, we can define
partial heaps

and
total heaps
and at a given world; the latter are used to
interpret the state monad of
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathit{ref}}
, whereas the former are used to
interpret the program logic. Partial heaps will be functorial in worlds,
whereas total heaps are a non-functorial derived form.

Definition 4.8
.

A
partial heap

h
at a world
w
is given by a finite subtype

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}\mathrel{\subseteq_{\textit{fin.}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
together with a heaplet

\eta_{h}:\mathcal{H}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}},w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}},w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}},w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}},w$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. We shall write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\triangleq w_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}}
for the supporting world; given
l\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}
we shall
write
hl
for
\eta_{h}l
. Partial heaps are arranged into a functor

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathsf{pH}:\mathbb{W}\to\mathsf{Set}}
; the covariant functoriality in worlds

w\leq w^{\prime}
takes a partial heap
h:\mathsf{pH}w
to
w^{\prime}_{*}h\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}},w^{\prime}_{*}\eta_{h}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}},w^{\prime}_{*}\eta_{h}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}},w^{\prime}_{*}\eta_{h}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}},w^{\prime}_{*}\eta_{h}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Definition 4.9
.

Two partial heaps are
disjoint
from each other when their
supports do not intersect. This property is both decidable and functorial
in
\mathbb{W}
, so it yields a decidable subobject of

\mathsf{pH}\times\mathsf{pH}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
. In the internal language of

\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
, we will write
h\#h^{\prime}
to mean that
h
and
h^{\prime}
are
disjoint.

Construction 7

We may define an internal
partial commutative monoid
structure
on
\mathsf{pH}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
. The unit is the empty heap
\emptyset
,
and the partial multiplication
h_{1}\cdot h_{2}
is defined when
h_{1}\#h_{2}

as follows:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}\cup\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}

\displaystyle\eta_{h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}}\ell

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{*}\eta_{h_{1}}\ell&\text{ if $\ell\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}$}\\
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}\cdot_{w}h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{*}\eta_{h_{2}}\ell&\text{ if $\ell\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{2}$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}$}\end{cases}

Definition 4.10
.

A
total heap
at a world
w
is a partial heap
h:\mathsf{pH}w
such that

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}=w
; this is not functorial in
\mathbb{W}
, but we may write total
heaps as a functor
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathsf{tH}_{\bullet}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\to\mathsf{Set}}

where
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
is the underlying discrete category of
\mathbb{W}
. We
shall abusively write
\mathsf{tH}
for the dependent sum

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{w:\mathbb{W}}}{\textstyle\sum_{w:\mathbb{W}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{w:\mathbb{W}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{w:\mathbb{W}}}\mathsf{tH}_{w}
that bundles a heap with its world. We will
write
\mathsf{tH}_{\#w}
to mean the type of total heaps whose support is
disjoint from
w
.

4.2.3
Semantic domains for predicates

Here we describe the semantic domains that govern predicates and entailments;
these domains will ultimately form the basis for a BI-hyperdoctrine
\mathcal{B}^{\bullet}

over
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
, to be described later. We shall denote by
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
the
Hofmann–Streicher lifting of
\mathsf{Prop}
into
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
, defining
\mathcal{B}
to be the internal poset of

\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-valued co-presheaves on the partial commutative monoid
\mathsf{pH}

under its extension order:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\\
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}w\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle w\downarrow\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Prop}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle w\downarrow\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Prop}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w\downarrow\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Prop}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w\downarrow\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Prop}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\end{array}\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\\
\mathcal{B}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}\end{array}

Notation 8

(Forcing for
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
)

For any
X:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
,
\phi:X\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
and
w:\mathbb{W}
and
x:Xw
, we
shall write
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}w\Vdash{\phi\,x}}
in
iGDTT
to mean that
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\phi_{w}x\,w}

holds.

Notation 9

(Forcing for
\mathcal{B}
)

Let
X:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
be a semantic type; then in the internal language of

\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
, for any
\phi:\mathcal{B}^{X}
,
h:\mathsf{pH}
, and
x:X
, we shall
write
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}h\models{\phi\,{x}}}
to mean that
{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\phi\,x\,h}
holds.

We note that
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
inherits
[
38
]
from
iGDTT

a later modality
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}
defined pointwise; it follows that
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
is
closed under a later modality
\rhd:\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
satisfying

w\Vdash{\rhd\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\Longleftrightarrow\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w\Vdash{\phi\,x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w\Vdash{\phi\,x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w\Vdash{\phi\,x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w\Vdash{\phi\,x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

4.2.4
Interpretation of judgmental structure

We summarize the interpretation of the judgmental structure of
Tulip

below:

(1)

Type contexts

\Xi\ \textit{tctx}

are interpreted as semantic
logical types
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathsf{Type}
.

(2)

Element contexts

\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}

are interpreted as
families
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

(3)

Types

\Xi\vdash A\ \textit{type}\mathrel{@}{\iota}

are interpreted as families

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\iota}
.

(4)

Elements

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash a:A

are interpreted as elements
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle .

(5)

Propositions

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}

are interpreted as
predicates
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\xi:\Xi}}{\textstyle\prod_{\xi:\Xi}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\xi:\Xi}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\xi:\Xi}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
.

(6)

Entailments

\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\phi\vdash\psi

are
interpreted as parameterized inequalities
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaysty .

4.2.5
Recursive types, general reference types, and the monad

In our semantics, recursive types are computed using the guarded domain
structure of the semantic universes
\mathcal{S}_{\iota}
; general
reference types are defined pointwise as a subtype of the world’s support; the
monad is likewise defined pointwise using a combination of universal types,
existential types, and the guarded lifting monad:

w^{\prime},h,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime},h,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\end{array}

Note that
\mathsf{set}_{A}
returns immediately in the guarded lift monad via the
unit
\eta
, whereas
\mathsf{get}_{A}
takes a single step via the
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}
-algebra
map
\vartheta
; this is because the heap stores its elements under the later
modality, so reading from memory takes one abstract step of computation in the
guarded lift monad. This is also reflected in the rule
wp-get
, which allows
an assumption to be under the later modality.

4.2.6
Semantics of logical types

We interpret the logical type of propositions
\mathtt{prop}
as the internal poset

\mathcal{B}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
. The interpretation of the remaining type
connectives is standard.

4.3
Semantics of predicate connectives

We will impose enough structure on
\mathcal{B}
such that the
indexed partial order
\mathcal{B}^{\bullet}:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathbf{Poset}_{\mathsf{Type}}
has the structure of a BI-hyperdoctrine with
appropriate modalities (
\square
,
\rhd
) and weakest preconditions.

4.3.1
A complete BI-algebra

We will argue that
\mathcal{B}
forms a
complete
BI-algebra
in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
. Note that in this section, when we say that a
partial order is
complete
, we mean that it is complete in the sense of
internal category theory
[
29
]
.

Lemma 4.11
.

\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
is a complete Heyting algebra in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

Corollary 4.12
.

The internal poset
\mathcal{B}
is a complete
Heyting algebra in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

We will use
Day’s convolution

[
19
,
20
]
to
construct a BI-algebra structure on
\mathcal{B}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
.
Day’s convolution product is most well-known for extending monoidal
structures on small categories to presheaves, but we will need the full
generality of his result: the categories involved are
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched, and
the structure on the base is
promonoidal
rather than
monoidal
.

Construction 10

(
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched promonoidal structure on a pcm)

A partial commutative monoid
M=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle M,\emptyset,\cdot$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle M,\emptyset,\cdot$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M,\emptyset,\cdot$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M,\emptyset,\cdot$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
can be viewed as a
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched category, because its extension order is valued in
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
. As a
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched category,
M
has
a
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched
promonoidal structure
in the sense of
Day
[
19
,
20
]
, which essentially encodes graph of the
partial multiplication operation:

(1)

The
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-distributor
\mathsf{mul}:M\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle M\times M$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle M\times M$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M\times M$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M\times M$}\mathclose{}{)}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}

sends
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n_{0},n_{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to
\exists n_{2}.m=n_{0}\cdot n_{1}\cdot n_{2}
.

(2)

The
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-distributor
\mathsf{unit}:{M}\times\mathbf{1}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
sends
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m,*$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to the
proposition
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle m=\emptyset$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle m=\emptyset$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle m=\emptyset$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle m=\emptyset$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

(3)

The associativity and unit isomorphisms are defined using the
associativity and unit laws for the partial multiplication operation.

Construction 11

(BI algebra)

We obtain a BI-algebra structure
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrel{\ast},\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrel{\ast},\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrel{\ast},\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\ast},\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
on
\mathcal{B}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
by taking the Day convolution of the induced

\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
-enriched promonoidal structure (
10
) on the
partial commutative monoid
\mathsf{pH}
, such that the separating conjunction
extends the partial multiplication operation on representables. In
particular, if
h\#h^{\prime}
are two disjoint partial heaps, then
\mathbf{y}h\mathrel{\ast}\mathbf{y}h^{\prime}=\mathbf{y}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h\cdot h^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h\cdot h^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h\cdot h^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h\cdot h^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

These constructions are explained in more detail for BI-algebras arising from
partial commutative monoids by Bizjak and
Birkedal
[
13
]
.

4.3.2
Modalities: persistence and later

The persistence modality is interpreted as the map
\square:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{B}

obtained by reindexing along the constant endomap
h\mapsto\emptyset
,
sending
\phi:\mathcal{B}
to
h\mapsto\phi\,\emptyset
. The later modality

\rhd:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{B}
is given
pointwise
.

4.3.3
The points-to predicate

We interpret the points-to predicate in the generic case

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\mid\ell:\mathtt{ref}\,A,a:A\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\mid\ell:\mathtt{ref}\,A,a:A\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\mid\ell:\mathtt{ref}\,A,a:A\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\mid\ell:\mathtt{ref}\,A,a:A\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
. In
particular, we must define for each program type
A:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}
a natural
transformation
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle-\hookrightarrow-$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle-\hookrightarrow-$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-\hookrightarrow-$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-\hookrightarrow-$}\mathclose{}{]}}:\mathsf{ref}\,A\times A\to\mathcal{B}
, which will
turn out to be representable by a singleton heap,
i.e
.
we may define

h\models{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}}\Longleftrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle l\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle l\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\leq h
.

4.3.4
Weakest preconditions

Finally we must interpret the weakest precondition connective, which we do in
the generic case of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\mid\phi:\alpha\to\mathtt{prop},u:\mathtt{T}{\alpha}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,u\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\mid\phi:\alpha\to\mathtt{prop},u:\mathtt{T}{\alpha}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,u\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\mid\phi:\alpha\to\mathtt{prop},u:\mathtt{T}{\alpha}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,u\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\mid\phi:\alpha\to\mathtt{prop},u:\mathtt{T}{\alpha}\vdash\mathtt{wp}\,u\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x.\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
. This amounts to constructing for each semantic program type

A:\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{p}}}
a natural transformation
\mathsf{wp}_{A}:\mathcal{B}^{A}\times\mathsf{T}{A}\to\mathcal{B}
. As the denotation of the state monad is defined world-by-world, so
must be the interpretation of
h\models{\mathsf{wp}_{A}\,u\,\phi}
; to that end, we
give the forcing clause for
w\Vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h\models{\mathsf{wp}_{A}\,u\,\phi}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h\models{\mathsf{wp}_{A}\,u\,\phi}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h\models{\mathsf{wp}_{A}\,u\,\phi}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h\models{\mathsf{wp}_{A}\,u\,\phi}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in
the external
iGDTT
language as follows, recalling the
\Downarrow
notation

\mathrel{\land}w_{r}\Vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}h^{\prime}\models{\phi_{w_{r}}\,a=\top}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}h^{\prime}\models{\phi_{w_{r}}\,a=\top}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}h^{\prime}\models{\phi_{w_{r}}\,a=\top}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w_{r}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{*}h^{\prime}\models{\phi_{w_{r}}\,a=\top}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Although the definition is quite technical, the idea is simple. The denotation
of a monadic program is a guarded-recursive process taking a heap and
ultimately producing a return configuration at a larger world. In simple terms,
the weakest precondition of a predicate
\phi
should quantify over all frames
for the starting heap and check that the process returns only configurations
satisfying
\phi
without disturbing the frame.

4.3.5
Explicit Kripke–Joyal translation

We have given the interpretation of our logic in a mostly abstract–categorical
way; such an abstract presentation verifies all the “logical” rules of our
system, but explicit computations are needed in order to verify the rules for
weakest preconditions. In this section, we provide some tools to assist with
these explicit computations; in
12
we describe how to
interpret each of the main connectives of the logic as a transformer of
subobjects in the internal language of
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

Computation 12

(Kripke–Joyal translation of the
\mathcal{B}

logic)

The action of each connective on
\mathcal{B}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{pH},\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
can be
computed explicitly as a forcing clause in the Kripke–Joyal
translation
[
35
]
. We omit the forcing clauses for

\top,\bot,\land,\lor,\exists,\rhd
because they are pointwise:

\displaystyle h\models{\phi\,x\Rightarrow\psi\,x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,h^{\prime}\models{\phi\,x}\Rightarrow h^{\prime}\models{\psi\,x}

\displaystyle h\models{\forall_{Y}\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{\prime}\geq h$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y:Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y:Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y:Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y:Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,h^{\prime}\models{\phi\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle h\models{\square\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\emptyset\models{\phi\,x}

\displaystyle h\models{\phi\,x\mathrel{\ast}\psi\,x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\exists\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h_{1}\cdot h_{2}=h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h_{1}\cdot h_{2}=h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{1}\cdot h_{2}=h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h_{1}\cdot h_{2}=h$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,h_{1}\models{\phi\,x}\land h_{2}\models{\psi\,x}

\displaystyle h\models{\phi\,x\mathrel{-\mkern-6.0mu\ast}\psi\,x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h^{\prime}\#h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle h^{\prime}\#h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h^{\prime}\#h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h^{\prime}\#h$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,h^{\prime}\models{\phi\,x}\Rightarrow h\cdot h^{\prime}\models{\psi\,x}

\displaystyle h\models{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle l\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle l\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle l\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle l\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell\mapsto\mathsf{next}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\leq h

Computation 13

(Kripke–Joyal translation of the
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
logic)

The connectives on
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
can be further computed in terms of the ambient

iGDTT
model by another layer of Kripke–Joyal forcing:

\displaystyle w\Vdash{\phi\,x\Rightarrow\psi\,x}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,w^{\prime}\Vdash{\phi\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}\Rightarrow w^{\prime}\Vdash{\psi\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle w\Vdash{\forall_{Y}\phi\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x,-$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\forall\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}\geq w$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y:Yw^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y:Yw^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y:Yw^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y:Yw^{\prime}$}\mathclose{}{)}}.\,w^{\prime}\Vdash{\phi\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime}_{*}x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime}_{*}x,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

4.4
Soundness results

The following results are stated internally to
iGDTT
.

Theorem 4.13

(Soundness)
.

If

\Xi\mid\Gamma\mid\phi\vdash\psi
is derivable in
Tulip
,
then for any
\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\ \textit{tctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\ \textit{tctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\ \textit{tctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\ \textit{tctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
it holds that

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\phi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi\leq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\psi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\psi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\psi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash\psi\ \textit{prop}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi
in

\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\vdash\Gamma\ \textit{ctx}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
.

Proof 4.14
.

Since
\mathcal{B}^{\bullet}
is a BI-hyperdoctrine, all the specified rules of higher-order
separation logic are valid
[
8
]
, and the
rules for the modalities follow similarly. What remains is to verify rules
for weakest preconditions; we show just one illustrative case in

4.15
.

.

. Then we instantiate

and

and

. Our remaining goal is

, which follows by instantiating
H_{2}
with
w^{\prime}\triangleq w^{\prime}
and
h_{3}\triangleq w^{\prime}_{*}h_{1}
.

Corollary 4.17

(Consistency)
.

It is not the case that
\top\leq\bot
in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
for
any
\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
.

5
Conclusions; related and future work

5.1
Comparison with operationally-based program logics

We have contributed a program logic
Tulip
over the equational theory of
polymorphic, general recursive, higher-order stateful programs by building on
the recent denotational semantics of general references and polymorphism of
Sterling
et al
.

[
46
]
, adapting many ideas that
were first developed in the context of operationally based program logics.
Prior works on the operational side such as Iris
[
30
]
, the Verified
Software Toolchain
[
1
]
, and
TaDA
[
18
]
have reached great
heights of expressivity, incorporating constructs such as invariants and
higher-order ghost state which are critical for reasoning about concurrent
programs.
For the sake of simplicity, we have restricted our attention to a
fixed

notion of resource (partial heaps), but we hope in the future to adapt more
sophisticated constructs including higher-order ghost state,
etc
.
to reach
parity with existing operationally-based program logics.

5.2
Other denotationally-based program logics for state

The model of Sterling
et al
.
is not the only denotational semantics of state.
First-order store, both local and global, is well-represented in the
literature
[
37
,
36
,
42
,
45
]
; there is
also Levy’s model of non-polymorphic higher-order store
[
33
]
, and
notably, a model of
local
full ground store
by
Kammar
et al
.

[
31
]
. Polzer and
Goncharov
[
43
]
have constructed a BI-hyperdoctrine over
the denotational semantics of Kammar
et al
.
, and our own work is much in the
spirit of theirs. However, there is an apparent mismatch between the semantics
of
local store
and the model of bunched implications over it, which has
impeded
op. cit
.
from developing a full program logic with an interpretation of
weakest preconditions.

5.3
Future perspectives

One of the methodological questions raised by our work is where, exactly, to
draw the line between equational reasoning and logical reasoning. For instance,
conventional operationally-based program logics do not use equational reasoning
at all: our logic, in contrast, allows some equational reasoning but it is
limited by the intensionality of Sterling
et al
.
’s model. One possible direction
for future work is to attempt to make the model itself less intensional, either
by enhancing the semantic worlds
[
22
]
or
by improving the interpretation of the state
monad
[
31
]
.

Another question is how
Tulip
can be implemented in a practical tool. Currently, definitional and logical equality coincide due to equality reflection, which leads the former to become undecidable. An implementation will therefore require a more refined account of the interaction between definitional and logical equality.

Omitted rules

Appendix B
Omitted proofs

Lemma

4.11
.

\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
is a complete Heyting algebra in
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
.

Proof B.1
.

The simplest way to see this is to embed
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{0}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

into the larger co-presheaf category
\mathscr{E}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Type}_{1}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
,
where we have a Hofmann–Streicher lifting
\mathcal{V}
of
\mathsf{Type}_{0}
whose global
points correspond to
\mathcal{S}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
. Then the
completeness
can be
expressed internally in terms of quantification over
\mathcal{V}
; this internal
quantification automatically satisfies the appropriate Beck–Chevalley
conditions when externalized. In particular, that
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}
has internal
products then amounts to the following pullback square
existing
[
3
]
:

\mathcal{V}
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{V}}}{\textstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{V}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{V}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{A:\mathcal{V}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
A\mapsto\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,\lambda\_.\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,\lambda\_.\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,\lambda\_.\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,\lambda\_.\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}
\mathbf{1}
\mathsf{Prop}_{\mathbb{W}}{}
!_{\mathcal{V}}
\top
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\forall$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\forall$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\forall$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\forall$}\mathclose{}{)}}

The lower map can be be constructed explicitly using the internal
completeness of
\mathsf{Prop}
in
\mathsf{Type}
. The rest of the Heyting algebra
structure is inherited from
\mathsf{Prop}
à la Kripke semantics over
\mathbb{W}
.

Corollary

4.17
.

It is not the case that
\top\leq\bot
in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
for
any
\xi:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
.

Proof B.2
.

The ordering on
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
is defined pointwise
in relative to ordering on
\mathcal{B}
, and its ordering is in turn defined
pointwise relative to the ordering on
\mathsf{Prop}
. Similarly,
\top
and
\bot

in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
are defined pointwise relative
to
\top
and
\bot
in
\mathcal{B}
, which in turn are defined pointwise relative
to
\top
and
\bot
in
\mathsf{Prop}
. Since
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi
is the
terminal object, and thus in particular is inhabited, this implies that if
\top\leq\bot
in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
, we also have
\top\leq\bot
in
\mathsf{Prop}
. We thus conclude
\top\not\leq\bot
in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathord{\cdot}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi}
.
